#2824. Giannis Antetokounmpo on facing Milwaukee: 'It’s going to be just another game'

Is anyone believing this? Including Giannis Antetokounmpo?

Wednesday night, the Miami Heat will host Milwaukee and face the Bucks, the first time that Antetokounmpo will play in another uniform against the team where he spent 13 seasons and won an NBA title. It's a game everyone had circled on the calendar, although the big one is Jan. 28 when Miami plays in Milwaukee.

However, when asked about it after the Heat's impressive win against the Rockets Monday night, Antetokounmpo tried to play it down as just another game.

"It's going to be just another game. I'm going to do the same thing. I'm going to eat the same meal. I'm going to kiss my kids before they go to bed, get ready, wake up, do my routine and get ready for the game. It's the same game. It's the same thing. It doesn't change. It doesn't matter the jersey...

"I haven't found myself being emotional. I think the only time I've been emotional was when we won the championship. I think I played my whole career, I haven't like found myself like putting emotion to the game. I feel like emotions are for movies, and this is just basketball."

He can say that because the game is in Miami, but Bucks fans are not going to feel the same way — this is emotional.

Antetokounmpo is the greatest Bucks player of all time and he put together a Hall of Fame resume while in Milwaukee: NBA champion and Finals MVP in 2021, two-time MVP, Defensive Player of the Year, nine All-NBA teams and 10 All-Star teams. He won the team its second NBA title, the first since the 1970s. Beyond the on-court success, he built a life in Milwaukee and invested in the city, doing charity work and more.

The exit got a little messy because of that investment — while there may have been basketball reasons for Antetokounmpo to ask for a trade earlier, he didn't want to be the bad guy forcing his way out of the only city and away from the only franchise he had known. Eventually, he felt he had to if he was going to win another ring, and he landed in Miami for a package of picks and players that will help the Bucks on the long road to rebuilding.

Antetokounmpo and Miami come to Milwaukee having found a groove and winning five straight, including beating a good Houston team to improve to 8-6 (good enough for the No. 4 seed in the East right now). Milwaukee is headed the opposite direction, having lost 10 straight.

It is going to be emotional on Wednesday, even if Antetokounmpo won't admit it.

#2820. Two players accept qualifying offers, other five decline

Today was the deadline for accepting the qualifying offer around MLB. Eight players were given a qualifying offer. Jake Bauers was the first to decline after signing a four-year contract with the Red Sox. The remaining seven players made their decision today. Two have accepted their offers and the other five have declined.

Ryan Jeffers is one of two players who was set to test free agency this offseason. The catcher decided to accept the offer and return to the Twins for the 2027 season. Jeffers has been with the Twins for his entire career. In 2026, he posted a .256/.353/.461 batting line with 14 home runs and 56 RBIs. Over his career, he has posted an 11.2 bWAR and 10.9 fWAR.

David Bednar was the other player that accepted the qualifying offer. The Yankees' closer was also set to test free agency for the first time, but also accepted the offer and will return for one more season. Bednar has a 2.47 ERA and 2.61 FIP in 2026, recording 35 saves in 64 games over 69 1/3 innings. He was acquired by the Yankees from the Pirates at the 2025 trade deadline and has recorded 146 saves over the last six seasons.

The other five players who received the qualifying offer have declined it. This includes Randy Arozarena of the Mariners, Seiya Suzuki of the Cubs, Trevor Rogers of the Orioles, Brandon Lowe of the Pirates, and Adrián Morejón of the Padres.

All five of these players are predicted for major pay days, per MLB Trade Rumors. Rogers is the second best starting pitcher on the market and projected to receive a five-year contract over $100 million. Suzuki and Arozarena are the best outfielders and also looking at deals around $100 million. Morejón and Lowe are looking at smaller contracts, but could get deals with AAVs over the $23.15 million they would have received by taking the qualifying offer.

Meanwhile, Jeffers and Bednar were also on track for major contracts, but not necessarily above the $23.15 million value they will receive from the qualifying offer. Jeffers was projected for a four-year contract at $75 million, while Bednar was projected for four years at $68 million. Both will take the guaranteed money from the qualifying offer and look for a bigger contract in the 2027-28 offseason.

The five players who declined the offer will now enter the free agent market fully. Their original team will get draft-pick compensation based on the contract that they sign. This will also affect the pick that the Brewers will receive for Bauers signing with the Red Sox, dependent on the other contracts signed this offseason. Here is the breakdown of how those picks work.

For teams who lost a player that declined the qualifying offer:

If the team is a revenue-sharing recipient and the player signs for $50 million or more, they will receive a pick after the first round of the draft and before Competitive Balance Round A. This is where the pick will fall for the Brewers from losing Bauers.

If the team is a revenue-sharing recipient and the player signs for less than $50 million, they will receive a pick after Competitive Balance Round B.

If the team paid the Competitive Balance Tax, their pick is after the fourth round, regardless of contract.

Any other team would receive a pick after Competitive Balance Round B, regardless of contract.

For teams who sign a player that declined the qualifying offer:

If the team is a revenue-sharing recipient, they will lose their third-highest pick. If they sign multiple, they will also lose their fourth-highest pick. There is no penalty to the international bonus pool.

If the team paid the Competitive Balance Tax, they will lose their second and fifth-highest picks, along with $1 million from their international bonus pool. If they sign multiple, they also lose their third and sixth-highest picks.

Any other team will lose their second-highest pick and $500,000 from their international bonus pool. If they sign multiple, they will also lose their third-highest pick.

#2813. Jeff Lebby's 2027 Mississippi State recruiting class has fourth decommit in a week

STARKVILLE – A fourth Mississippi State football player since Nov. 10 decommitted from the Bulldogs' 2027 recruiting class. This time, it was the Bulldogs' top-ranked recruit.

Yoshawn Hudson, a four-star running back from Winona, announced his decommitment on Nov. 17. The Clarion Ledger Dandy Dozen selection had been committed to MSU since June 14.

"After much consideration and prayer, I am taking this opportunity to share that I am decommitting from Mississippi State," Hudson wrote in an X post. "I'd like to thank Coach (Jeff) Lebby, (running backs) Coach (Anthony) Tucker, and the rest of the Mississippi State coaches for the opportunity to play for the Bulldogs. I'd also like to thank the Mississippi State fan base for their support. This wasn't an easy decision."

Since Nov. 10, four-star offensive tackle Li'Marcus Jones, three-star safety Hudson Fuqua and three-star cornerback Julian McDonald have also departed the recruiting class. However, the Bulldogs have also received two commitments from junior college players since then: three-star defensive lineman Jekai Lee and unranked tight end Jourdan Butler.

The Bulldogs (4-6) are on a six-game losing streak in Lebby's third season. They play Tennessee Tech (9-2) at Davis Wade Stadium on Nov. 21 (noon, SEC Network+).

Mississippi State's 15-player recruiting class was ranked No. 57 before Hudson's decommitment, according to the 247Sports Composite, last in the SEC and on pace to be the lowest ranked in program history since rankings began in 2000.

MORE: Why Jeff Lebby fired 2 Mississippi State assistant coaches a game after saying he wouldn't

Jones is the second highest-ranked recruit who decommitted, but he was only in MSU's class for one day. The former Callaway player who now plays for Brentwood Academy (Tennessee) flipped from Colorado to Mississippi State only to recommit to Colorado.

"After having a hard time sleeping and thinking on my decision I realize that going back home to Mississippi is not for me," Jones wrote in an X post. "I allowed the media to get to my head about where Coach Prime (Deion Sanders) will be at next year but the staff informed me that they will in fact be their so with that being said I'm an 100% locked in with Coach Prime and the buffs."

Fuqua committed to Mississippi State in November 2025 and McDonald on June 8. They have not committed to new teams.

Four-star quarterback Gavin Hampton became the highest-ranked player in Mississippi State's 2027 class after Hudson's decommitment. There are three other four-stars: safety Kennedy Green, running back Christian Alexander and defensive lineman Jaden Bayonne.

#2812. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

#2810. Pistons vs Spurs preview: No Duncan or Duren, others must step up

The Detroit Pistons are down four rotation players as they match up with Tobias Harris and the San Antonio Spurs. Harris has not made his season debut due to a nagging calf injury. That hasn't affected the Spurs in the slightest.

The absences of Duncan Robinson, Ron Holland, Isaiah Joe, and Jalen Duren surely make a difference for Detroit. The shooters will be extremely missed, with Victor Wembanyama's presence shrinking the floor.

Duren has some eye-popping moments against the MVP candidate, but we won't see that big man clash until late March, unless they line it up in the NBA cup Finals.

Where: Frost Bank Center, San Antonio, TX

There aren't too many games where Cade Cunningham doesn't step on the floor as the guy. He can be him in any matchup, but it will take a lot to top Wemby. Considering how small Detroit is currently, their hands are full with the 7-foot-4 Frenchman.

John Collins started at the 5 over the last three games. With Robinson out, don't be surprised if Paul Reed re-enters the starting group. That isn't a Goliath pairing, but some teams opt for as much size as possible when defending Wemby.

The "put the small on Wemby" philosophy hasn't been fruitful this year. Wemby is dunking everything. Per Basketball-Reference, 27 percent of his shots are slams. That's way up from 18 percent last year. Detroit could have success "forcing" him to shoot. He's shooting 32 percent on triples and 39 percent on all middies.

That's a tough ask. Detroit's defense came up short against Giannis Antetokounmpo and the Heat Saturday night. Giannis scored 24 points in the paint. That same type of shots-at-the-rim challenge is up tonight.

Here's a glass-half-full takeaway from Collins. He's made at least two 3s in each of the past three games. Yes, glass half full for sure. Plus, he is shooting 38 percent from 3 on the season. Teams haven't cared, though. They still treat him like a maybe shooter. San Antonio will absolutely dare him to shoot from the wings tonight.

Detroit needs the open ones. Collins has to start hitting enough for teams to feel they need to stay attached. The defensive rotations can get better. The rim protection will not get better. That's not who he is. He's been disappointing defensively and on the glass. The shooting has to be feared enough to aid the spacing — high percentages on low volume won't shift defenses.

The Spurs are the second-best defense in the league, only trailing the Minnesota Timberwolves. While Wemby suffocates you, the "smalls" relentlessly hound.

Stephon Castle and Dylan Harper are big guards who get after it. Castle especially is an All-Defense caliber guy. Cade plays to win, but he'll want to let loose against Castle, who gave him trouble in the second February game last year.

Cade was better in the first one and led Detroit to the dub. He'll try to do that again tonight with an undermanned roster.

Ausar Thompson will do some leading too, as he's had the ball in his hands a lot more. According to Databallr, here's his on-ball percentage by year:

Ausar got his first triple-double a few games ago. He was so involved, zipping up and down the floor. Detroit needs all of that and then some tonight. He has to stay on the floor.

In the dawg category, Daniss Jenkins is in the 100th percentile. He steps up whenever Detroit is counted out. No Robinson, so he'll surely start. The Spurs don't allow many 3s, so Jenkins needs to be ready to knock down when he gets his chances.

Jenkins creates a lot of chances with his I don't care who's guarding me mentality, so expect him to get them up, with the paint being limited.

You can't fault his philosophy either. Jenkins is shooting 42 percent on five 3s a game. He makes them count. Detroit will need every ounce of his fearlessness as they travel to San Antonio. The Spurs are winners of 12 in a row. Detroit will rarely be nine-point underdogs. Let's see how they come out in this position.

Detroit Pistons (8-4): Cade Cunningham, Daniss Jenkins, Ausar Thompson, John Collins, Paul Reed

San Antonio Spurs (12-2): De'Aaron Fox, Dylan Harper, Stephon Castle, Devin Vassell, Victor Wembanyama

Who or what has been the most impressive in the No-Duren stretch?

#2808. Week 11 Power Rankings Round Up: Packers come back down to earth

Well, Green Bay's matchup against Minnesota went about as badly as it could have, as the Packers fell 30-10. As it turns out, relying on Jordan Love to play at superhuman levels has its drawbacks. Love has answered the call at every turn and played admirably, all things considered, in Week 10, but it turns out that football is a team game, and offensive line play is an important part of that team.

As doom and gloom as Sunday was, there were some positives. J. Michael Sturdivant caught his first career touchdown, Kaleb Johnson showed some flashes when he had time to run, and Jordan Love finally drew a roughing the passer penalty, resulting in probably the loudest cheer of the day aside from the pregame flyover.

It really has been a drastic swing of emotions for the green and gold faithful. There has been some really ugly football, some incredibly good football as we saw in New England, and then a swing back to really bad in Week 10. As much as people blame the offensive line, it truly was a team effort, or lack thereof. Green Bay's defense gave up a touchdown on a 3rd and 23, Trey Smack missed another field goal, the line did let up 8 sacks, and the team coughed up 3 fumbles to go with a Jordan Love interception.

In last week's various Power Rankings, many had the Packers inside the Top 10, with Sports Illustrated having the team as high as the 5 spot in its rankings. One week later, Green Bay still remains in the top half of the league, perhaps as a testament to the strength of the Vikings. Or perhaps it is due to the Packers still finding themselves a playoff team, a true reminder that one game isn't the be-all and end-all in the grand scheme of things.

Thankfully, Green Bay will not lose this week, as the bye week is finally here. Every year, it seems like the bye week comes at the perfect time, but this year, that really rings true. It will be the shortest bye week in the NFL, as both the Rams and Packers will come off their respective bye weeks to play on Thanksgiving Eve a week from Wednesday. Josh Jacobs could be back, Micah Parsons should be back, and Kevin Zeitler will have another week to heal up and ramp up. So with the bye week here, let's see where Green Bay stands around the league:

NFL.com's Nick Shook has the Packers as the highest mark on the collective lists this week, keeping Green Bay at the 10th spot, dropping 2 spots from the 8th spot. Shook writes:

"We're now entering the edge of the quagmire. Green Bay's reliance on the explosive play became a weakness against Minnesota, as a lack of sustainable success contributed to its 20-point loss to the division rival. One might simply say the Vikings have the Packers' number; I tend to think Minnesota merely exposed a weakness that might hold back Green Bay as it fights to remain in the playoff hunt in the toughest division in football. That's not enough to knock the Pack out of the top 10, but it does shake my confidence in them."

Suffice to say, Green Bay is still viewed relatively favorably around the league. Despite falling 7 spots in Conor Orr's rankings on Sports Illustrated, the Packers still hold the 12 spot in his metrics. So it certainly could be worse. Week 11 will kick off in a couple of days, and with Green Bay sitting idle on the bye week this week, they do not control whether they rise or fall. Enjoy the week of stress-free football!

#2803. NBA Panic Meter: Can Pelicans fix this mess? Do Suns need to make a move?

We're about a month into the season, the perfect time to check in on some underperforming situations around the league. Are these overreactions given the small sample sizes? Or are there deeper things at play that will continue to be magnified? Let's take a look.

Stop me if you've heard this before, but New Orleans is a mess.

Players taking laps around the court in the midst of a near-60-point loss. Cryptic messages in pressers. Star players retweeting reports of a trade request. (I'm not joking, this is the craziest thing I've ever seen since covering this sport.)

ESPN broke news of Dejounte Murray's wishes to depart from the franchise, but the growing consensus among rival executives is that Murray's value has gotten to a point where he cannot be moved unless there is quality draft compensation attached to him, sources told Yahoo Sports. The whispers of growing discontent behind the scenes related to the accelerated hires of head coach Jamahl Mosley (this season) and by proxy, executive vice president of basketball operations Joe Dumars (last season), had only grown louder amid the Pelicans' recent six-game losing streak (they ended the streak Monday, defeating the Steph Curry-less Warriors), sources said, citing confusion as to why extensive searches were not conducted on either front.

(Henry Russell/Yahoo Sports Illustration)

As it pertains to Murray, according to sources, the thinking is simple. His unhappiness and hope for a new environment is not new and has been discussed privately since last season, with a growing sense that his reluctance to return earlier from his January 2025 Achilles tear was in hopes of being moved at last year's deadline. But it was rather difficult to define a market for an injured player (Murray returned later that month). The decision to draft Jeremiah Fears last summer was another clear indicator that New Orleans was open to moving in another direction, which sources said upset Murray — echoing similar sentiments to his departures from San Antonio and Atlanta.

"What a f***ing mess," one high-ranking executive from a rival team said. "No one wants to touch him."

The veteran guard is a polarizing talent when healthy and engaged, but finding the harmony of both has been quite the challenge over his decade-long career.

The Suns opting to go ZeroPG in their starting lineup (word to Matt Harmon) is going about exactly as you might have expected. Phoenix started the season losing five of their first six games and is currently a game under .500. But it hasn't been its 25th-ranked defense that has been the talk of the town. It's the utter lack of cohesion and control at the other end.

A glance at metrics would show you the Suns actually are 11th in overall (and half-court) offensive efficiency, which is juiced somewhat, thanks to some funky transitional opportunities (the Suns are third in points off turnovers because of an aggressive on-ball scheme that generates mistakes). They're just outside the bottom 10, though, in passing rate, assist points created and potential assists.

And they're losing the Jalen Green-at-point-guard minutes badly. Phoenix has been outscored by 137 points in the 346 minutes Green has been on the floor, according to PBP Stats. Quite simply, it hasn't gone to plan. When Green was initially traded as part of the blockbuster move sending Kevin Durant to Houston, sources say one of Green's reasons for optimism was the consistent word from the Suns that he would be given on-ball opportunities — an element of his game that gradually faded with the emergence of Amen Thompson. The numbers, at least in theory, backed it up; Green was a low-turnover, high-usage guy that showed promise in spots as a creator when called upon.

But part of the charm with Green's playmaking was it came within the flow of his aggression, utilizing his speed and power. Factor into some offseason roster moves, notably inserting Miles Bridges (who isn't one to turn down a drive), more reliance on Devin Booker and less on Green, and you have yourself a player being shoehorned into a role that doesn't suit him or the team. Green's drive rate is the lowest it's been in four seasons and he's converting shots and holding on to the ball at career lows. (He's also shooting around 64% from the charity stripe this month and has yet to crack 30% from deep.) And it's led league executives to believe the 24-year-old, with one year left on his deal and a $36 million player option for next year, is attainable — and that Green wouldn't be opposed to a better fit elsewhere. It's unclear at this stage what Green's current market looks like, but New Orleans, which has kept tabs on him since he was in Houston, just might be paying more attention.

I'll admit, this might feel a tad bit nitpicky.

The Heat are riding a five-game winning streak, have won seven of their last nine, and sit just one game off third in the East after a horrendous start to the season. Klay Thompson has effectively doubled his scoring output this month, Miami just dropped 137 on a top-10 defensive unit and the Giannis Antetokounmpo-Bam Adebayo tandem seems to be working out some early kinks.

The Heat are a stone's throw outside the bottom-five in defensive rating, 29th in opponent 3-point and midrange conversion rate, and 29th in half-court defense.

With any metric — especially this early — some noise is expected. We also don't typically have a decent grasp on a team until about 15-20 games. So yes, there will be some bad opponent shooting luck.

But part of Miami's selling point as a contender was the versatility of Antetokounmpo, Adebayo and Davion Mitchell, and some of those concessions meant assuming it was its offense, not defense, that would need time to jell. Given the uneven start, two central questions surrounding Miami's defensive output are: have the schemes changed since Giannis' arrival, and is this working?

For starters, the Heat are throwing out a lot less zone than normal. Last season, nearly 17% of their defensive possessions featured a zone defense, which has dropped over 10%(!), according to Synergy tracking data. (Miami was also allowing a little less than a point per possession on such instances, now sitting closer to league average.) They're switching more than normal but not getting the same results: bottom 10 in points allowed (that's not solely a Giannis/Bam issue, but they're not fully absolved).

Looking more closely, issues remain with that tandem. Opponents are shying away from the rim when both are on the floor (in their most-used lineup), but the ones that dare to attack are converting over 70% of chances. In addition, when they give up 3s, opponents are shooting 50% from deep. Hardly the recipe for success. Antetokounmpo and Adebayo are also contesting less, not getting enough ball pressure and just not looking aggressive in passing lanes (their "Big 3" on defense are hovering around 50% in steals and deflection rates).

I'm not overly worried, but I'm also cooling down on my prediction that this will end up as a top-five defense by season's end.

I understand why everyone (including this committee of one) was so high on Charlotte heading into the year. There was always going to be a risk in trading LaMelo Ball and his cerebral playmaking, undoubtedly the heartbeat of one of the NBA's premier offenses — but the Hornets seemed equipped to handle it. Charles Lee, one of the brightest young minds around, was still in charge. So were Brandon Miller and Kon Knueppel, to whom the keys were to be given, as well as Naz Reid's unicorn floor spacing and Coby White's tantalizing projections as lead ball-handler.

So how does this team find itself five games under .500 and ranked 30th in offense and half-court efficiency?

A few reasons. Knueppel has played just six minutes, re-aggravating the same left hamstring that kept him sidelined during the preseason. White just returned this month after suffering a calf strain and continues to be on a minutes restriction. Miller has shown no confidence in handling the lion's share of touches ceded by Ball and Miles Bridges, averaging the highest usage and lowest shot-making efficiency of his career thus far, making just 28 of 110 3-point attempts.

And the misuse of Reid has been puzzling to say the least. Over his last three seasons in Minnesota, Reid's 3-point volume accounted for around half of his offensive usage. That has been sliced down to less than 40%, which ranks in just the 34th percentile among forwards, with only 7% of his attempts coming from the corners (14th percentile). It's been a work in progress to say the least, which explains why Reid's frustration, combined with Lee's no-nonsense approach, led to a benching earlier in the month.

But this is as much of a problem of Lee's as it is the new pieces in place. The Hornets have become too predictable and stagnant, not enough rim presence and no real control, either. Being one of the best offensive rebounding and worst ball-security teams is like taking two steps forward and four back. Getting Knueppel back will solve quite a bit — as will simplifying Miller's role and expectations — but it doesn't look good in Charlotte right now.

#2800. Weekly Recap 2: Hawks take a step back

We are just shy of the season being a month old, the Atlanta Hawks find themselves as the number four team in the Eastern Conference at 8-6. Despite being one of the better teams in the conference, this past week may have been a step back for a team that found themselves as winners of four straight.

The week's first matchup was against the Los Angeles Lakers at home. The Lakers were winners of five straight heading into Monday's game in Atlanta and were led by Luka Doncic, who was averaging more than 40 points per game at the time. The Hawks' roster is a near ideal group to slow down Doncic's historic pace with multiple versatile defenders on the perimeter. Dyson Daniels drew the assignment of Doncic, which was not a surprise given his reputation as one of the best on-ball defenders in the league.

While Daniels began the night containing Doncic from one of his signature 20-point first quarters, the perennial All-NBA guard displayed his other-worldly passing ability, as he racked up five assists during the opening period, which opened up the Lakers' offense and led them to an early nine-point lead which they did not relinquish for the rest of the game.

Unfortunately for the Hawks, they were plagued by turnovers and poor interior play. Lakers' center Walker Kessler was a matchup nightmare for the Lakers, as he shot 8-for-12 from the field on his way to 21 points and ten rebounds. Kessler's contributions were a significant factor in the Hawks surrendering 52 points in the paint. The poor performance down low has become not just a trend, but a full-blown habit for the Hawks, as they currently allow the third most points in the paint in the entire league.

The Hawks also turned the ball over 20 times, which the Lakers turned into 29 points, as opposed to the Hawks turning their forced turnovers into 14 points. Despite these discrepancies, the Hawks were still in the game late, trailing by only two with just under seven minutes to go. Dyson Daniels was making life tough for Luka Doncic and anyone else he guarded, as he picked up four steals along the way, which has become a signature for the league's leading pickpocket thief.

Even with his strong performance defensively, Doncic proved to be too much for the Hawks down the stretch, as he waved off multiple screens while being guarded by Daniels, which then turned into back-breaking buckets.

The Hawks' two leading scorers, Jalen Johnson and 'NAW' struggled mightily from the field, combining for 12-for-33 shooting, which just simply put is not good enough to beat most teams, let alone a squad that is clicking on all cylinders like the Lakers. Even with CJ McCollum and Aaron Wiggins having big nights off the benches, the poor outing from Johnson and 'NAW' was too much to overcome.

Luckily, the Hawks got their toughest test of the week out of the way with the Lakers. Their remaining two opponents, the Chicago Bulls and Milwaukee Bucks each only had a single win apiece. Smooth sailing, right?

The first matchup of the two was against the Bulls, who would be without their phenom rookie, Caleb Wilson. What should have been a sure-fire win against an undermanned Bulls team turned into a frigid display of three-point shooting from the Hawks that made winning quite difficult.

Frigid may be an understatement for a night that featured them shooting 8-for-40 from behind the arc, which equaled an abysmal 20%. The poor shooting especially plagued them in the overtime period, shooting 0-for-6 and totally stifling their offense.

The biggest star for the Bulls was the newly-acquired Norman Powell, who tallied a season-high 33 points on an impressive 12-for-19 shooting. Josh Giddey also had a triple-double, which included a staggering 14 assists.

On the Hawks side, Jalen Johnson also had a triple-double, which was his first of the season. Despite notching another triple-double under his belt, the feat was outweighed by his poor 11-for-26 shooting night along with five turnovers. CJ McCollum had another productive night off the bench with 20 points and Onyeka Okongwu also chipped in 17 points.

However, these contributions couldn't overcome an unprecedentedly bad shooting night for the Hawks, as they outperformed the Bulls in major categories such as forced turnovers, points in the paint, and fastbreak points.

The week concluded against the Bucks whose poor season has made them perhaps the runaway choice as the worst team in the entire league. Despite their woes of the season so far, they seemed to be the Hawks' second consecutive upset, as the Bucks raced out to 37-18 at the end of the first quarter. The deficit was attributed to red-hot deep shooting from the Bucks and a continued drought from behind the arc from the Hawks.

Luckily for the Hawks, the Bucks did what they do best- get in their own way. The teams essentially traded the shooting trends in the first quarter in the following period, as the Hawks claimed the lead headed into halftime. While the Hawks quickly raced out to a double-digit lead in the third, their shooting struggles returned, as they shot 28% from three in the second half.

Both teams had a sluggish second half, as the periods were plagued by bad shots, sloppy passes, and defensive lapses, which looked more like a bad day of pick-up ball at your local rec center instead of an NBA game. Luckily for the Hawks, the Bucks' worst day is probably the worst day for any team in the league.

Despite late surges from the likes of Gary Trent Jr., whose most recent contract was so inconceivable that it summoned an investigation from the league, the Hawks held on for the win with final score at 113-106.

Luckily for the Hawks, 'NAW' cleaned up his shooting struggles, leading the way with 20 points on 7-for-15 shooting. CJ McCollum also put up 20 points, which included shooting a perfect 9-for-9 from the free throw line and some clutch buckets down the stretch as the game neared its end.

Despite the win, the continued lackadaisical passing, poor shooting, and poor rebounding from the game are trends that look to make the rest of the season a struggle for the Hawks. As I mentioned last week, this long season will not be won or lost in November.

However, poor habits can compound quickly and send teams on a downhill slope that could be tough to recover from. With both the Oklahoma City Thunder and Detroit Pistons both on the docket this week, the margin for error will be much smaller than the competition they faced last week at the hands of the Bulls and Bucks.

#2797. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2796. Ukraine damages Putin’s prized Crimea bridge as Zelenskyy lauds ‘unprecedented operation’

Ukraine has damaged the Kerch Bridge linking Russia to the occupied Crimean Peninsula, delivering a symbolic blow to President Vladimir Putin as Kyiv reels from strikes on its own infrastructure.

Sergey Aksyonov, the Russia-installed governor of Crimea, confirmed that the strategically important bridge, which had been briefly closed on Monday, was attacked overnight for the second time in two days.

Videos and photos circulating on social media appeared to show the partial destruction of the bridge, with one section having collapsed into the water. CNBC was unable to independently verify the footage.

Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy described the attack as an "unprecedented operation" several weeks in the making, with Ukraine's defense forces, security services and weapons manufacturers involved.

"Russia strikes our infrastructure every day, including bridges in Zaporizhzhia, Dnipro, Kyiv, and the Odesa region," Zelenskyy said Tuesday on X.

"We are restoring our infrastructure. But we are also reminding Russia that war will always return to where it began. Ukraine's defensive capabilities are strong and will remain so, and we will never leave terror against our cities and people unanswered," he added.

Train and vehicle traffic was suspended due to the attack. No injuries have been reported.

Ukraine has repeatedly targeted the bridge since Russia launched its full-scale invasion in early 2022.

A 12-mile crossing of road and railways, the Kerch Bridge is one of Putin's signature projects. The crossing, sometimes referred to as the Crimean bridge, was built on his orders shortly after the Kremlin forcibly annexed Crimea in 2014 to support Moscow's claims to the territory.

Rail traffic on the bridge was opened on one track, Russia's Ministry of Transport said in a statement via Telegram on Tuesday, while restoration work continues on the second section.

The co-owner and chief engineer of Ukrainian missile and drone maker Fire Point, Denys Shtilierman, appeared to confirm the company's role in the attack, following reports that a Ukrainian Flamingo cruise missile was used in the strike.

"We dedicate this to the memory of our friends and colleagues Andriy and Yuriy, without whom this would not have been possible. R.I.P.," Shtilierman said via X on Tuesday. A spokesperson at Fire Point separately confirmed the company's role in the attack.

Analysts at the Institute for the Study of War, a Washington-based think tank, said Ukraine's attacks on the Kerch Bridge were likely part of Kyiv's ongoing and systematic strike campaign aimed at denying Russian forces the use of major ground lines of communication via Crimea.

Russian forces, meanwhile, struck two bridges crossing the Dnipro River in Kyiv.

Vitaliy Klitschko, the mayor of Ukraine's capital, said via Telegram on Tuesday that specialists were working to assess the extent of the damage to the North and South bridges.

Klitschko said Russian attacks on Kyiv overnight had also caused disruptions to water and heating supplies.

The strikes come ahead of Putin's trip to China this week for the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation forum in Shenzhen, where a trilateral meeting with U.S. President Donald Trump and Chinese President Xi Jinping is planned on Thursday.

Asked whether Ukraine's latest attacks might be connected to the upcoming trilateral summit, Kremlin spokesperson Dmitry Peskov said this could not be ruled out.

"Combat operations continue, and our military is striking targets that are somehow connected to Ukraine's military-industrial complex. And Ukraine is continuing its terrorist attacks," Peskov said, according to Russian state news outlet Ria Novosti.

#2795. College Football TV Ratings: Tracking the most-watched games of the 2026 season

Last year, college football saw its TV ratings surge and looked to keep the trend going in 2026. On3 is tracking the most-watched games of the season, according to Nielsen Big Data + Panel data compiled weekly.

Through the first four weeks, Texas' thrilling comeback win over Ohio State emerged as the top game of the college football season. It was the first game of the year to hit 10 million viewers, averaging nearly 14 million as part of the Week 2 slate.

$19.99 gets you a FULL year of On3 | Rivals national coverage

Here is a look at the Top 10 most-watched games of the 2026 season as of Week 9, which will be updated weekly. Additionally, a TV ratings tracker is below the Top 10 with viewership for every Nielsen-rated college football game of the year.

Note: SEC Network, ACC Network and CBS Sports Network do not pay for Nielsen to measure viewership.

Date/Time: Sept. 12, 7:30 p.m. ET (Week 2)Channel: ABCViewers: 13.99 million

The biggest game of Week 2 delivered for ABC. Texas' thrilling comeback win over Ohio State became the most-watched game on the schedule with 14.0 million viewers, which was down from last year's game, which brought 16.6 million on FOX.

The Longhorns rallied from a 23-3 deficit entering the fourth quarter to get the 24-23 win over the then-top ranked Buckeyes. Arch Manning threw for a touchdown in that final frame while Hollywood Smothers ran in two scores, including the eventual game-winner.

Date/Time: Oct. 10, 7:30 p.m. ET (Week 6)Channel: ABCViewers: 12.24 million

ABC's primetime game in Week 6 delivered in a big way. Georgia and Alabama went down to the wire at Bryant-Denny Stadium before the Bulldogs came away with the 20-17 victory in the most-watched game of the week – and the second most-watched of the season, per data compiled by On3.

Gunner Stockton went 18-for-22 for 249 yards and a touchdown, to one interception, while also adding a rushing touchdown to lead the charge. Georgia also jumped up to No. 1 in the AP Top 25 as a result of the win, coupled with Texas' loss to Oklahoma.

Date/Time: Sept. 19, 7:30 p.m. ET (Week 3)Channel: ABCViewers: 10.90 million

The top draw in Week 3 delivered as the most-watched game on the schedule. Ole Miss' victory over LSU averaged 10.9 million viewers on ABC to rank as the second-most watched game of the year, according to data compiled by On3.

The game was one of the most anticipated matchups of the year as Lane Kiffin made his return to Oxford for the first time since leaving Ole Miss last season. Trinidad Chambliss stole the show as he completed 33 of 48 passes for 363 yards and two touchdowns in the air, and he ran in what proved to be the game-winning touchdown with 6:14 to go in the fourth quarter.

Date/Time: Oct. 17, 12 p.m. ET (Week 7)Channel: FOXViewers: 10.38 million

In a hotly contested matchup, it appeared as though Indiana was going to avoid the upset against Ohio State. However, Nick Marsh was out of bounds when he caught the go-ahead touchdown, and the Buckeyes prevailed on FOX in the most-watched game of Week 7.

With the win, Ohio State ended Indiana's 22-game win streak, dating back to last season. However, the Buckeyes saw quarterback Julian Sayin go down with an injury that Ryan Day said could keep him out "several weeks."

Date/Time: Oct. 7, 3:30 p.m. ET (Week 6)Channel: ABCViewers: 9.82 million

The Red River Rivalry provided great theatre in Week 6 as Oklahoma rallied to take down Texas. Tate Sandell sent the game-winning field goal through the uprights as time expired, sealing the Sooners' victory over the Longhorns.

It marked Texas' first loss of the season after a 4-0 start to the year. Oklahoma, meanwhile, got above .500 after two losses prior to the bye week while the defense contained Arch Manning and the Longhorns offense.

Date/Time: Sept. 26, 12 p.m. ET (Week 4)Channel: ABCViewers: 9.7 million

Defense was the name of the game in Texas' victory over Tennessee at Neyland Stadium. The matchup led off the Week 4 slate and brought a TV ratings win for ABC in its early college football slot as the most-watched game of the week.

Considered the top game of the noon window, Texas' victory brought in 9.7 million viewers for ABC to lead the Week 4 slate. Colin Simmons set a Longhorns program record with 5.5 sacks as Texas held off a late Vols push to get the road win.

Date/Time: Oct. 17, 7:30 p.m. ET (Week 7)Channel: ABCViewers: 9.16 million

The headliner of the Week 7 primetime slate, Notre Dame and BYU squared off in a highly anticipated Top-10 matchup at LaVell Edwards Stadium. The Cougars fought off a late charge, though, and got the win in ABC's most-watched game of Week 7.

Bear Bachmeier had a huge game with two rushing touchdowns, a passing touchdown and a receiving touchdown to lead the way. On the Notre Dame side, CJ Carr led the Irish down the field before a final touchdown pass attempt fell incomplete.

Date/Time: Nov. 14, 7:30 p.m. ET (Week 11)Channel: ABCViewers: 9.06 million

ABC's primetime game delivered in a big way Texas and LSU came down to the wire. But with seconds to go, Arch Manning found Raleek Brown for the game-winning touchdown to seal the Longhorns' win in Baton Rouge.

Manning led a game-winning drive after Texas got the ball back with just over two minutes to go in regulation. The matchup became the most-watched game of Week 11 with 9.06 million viewers tuning in for the showdown.

Date/Time: Nov. 7, 3:30 p.m. ET (Week 10)Channel: ABCViewers: 8.86 million

The headliner of Saturday's late-afternoon window saw Alabama take on LSU at Tiger Stadium with plenty at stake for both teams. It went back-and-forth to start, but the Crimson Tide seized control in the second half to get the 31-13 victory.

Alabama rattled off 21 unanswered points after LSU took a 13-10 lead as the Tide got a key road victory to get November underway. ABC's late-afternoon window continues to see success through the 2026 season, as well.

Date/Time: Nov. 7, 7:30 p.m. ET (Week 10)Channel: NBCViewers: 8.48 million (9.1 million including Adobe Analytics/Peacock)

In a rematch of last year's season opener, Notre Dame vs. Miami was one of the top games in Week 10. The Fighting Irish got their revenge with the 31-24 victory in what became the most-watched game of Week 10.

With the win, Notre Dame bolstered its College Football Playoff resume after debuting at No. 11 in the initial Top 25 rankings last week. It was also a big night for NBC and Peacock, which led the primetime window in Week 10 with their combined viewership across Nielsen Big Data + Panel and Adobe Analytics.

In 2025, college football saw TV ratings increase 4% during the regular season and a 9% uptick in minutes consumed, counting the postseason. The College Football Playoff also saw ratings surge in the first year of Nielsen's Big Data + Panel reporting method, as well. This season provides a chance for more of an apples-to-apples comparison.

#2794. Ranking 8 potential trade targets for the Phoenix Suns

If you're a Suns fan, you've probably noticed all the trade rumors surrounding the team since their disastrous 1-5 start to the season. You also probably noticed that they're getting absolutely annihilated by the playoff-caliber teams that they play, most recently the Lakers and the Spurs.

We knew going into the season that the team lacked a viable point guard other than Collin Gillespie, and that the only true power forward on the team was Rasheer Fleming. Analysts point out that rebounding and three-point shooting were also likely to be an issue. The hope was that someone would step up and fill those roles.

Unfortunately, the guys that needed to step up and address those weaknesses have not, and probably cannot. Jalen Green has been a great teammate and obviously been trying to buy into Coach Ott's system, but it's obvious to all observers he's not able to fill the role of a point guard and is a terrible fit next to Booker. Collin Gillespie had a miserable start to the season, shooting only 34.1% from the field through the team's first 11 games. Rasheer Fleming has been exiled to the deep bench while looking slow and lost when he does play. Miles Bridges is a good rebounder…for a small forward…but struggles against the taller players he's expected to compete with on the boards.

There have been unconfirmed rumors of disgruntlement in the locker room over the lack of a point guard. When Dejounte Murray made a trade demand, rumors immediately swirled that the Suns were interested. Even John "The Trade Reaper" Gambodoro has admitted the Suns are likely going to be looking for defensive rebounders starting in late January.

Complicating matters for most rumors, though, is that the Suns do not have many assets to move. Most of their future draft picks have already been traded away until the early 20230's (ouch). Only a few players on the roster are trade-eligible now: chief among them Jalen Green, Ryan Dunn, Khaman Maluach, Koa Peat, and Miles Bridges. Mark Williams won't be trade-eligible until January 15, 2027. Dunn is playing exceptionally well, and Bridges is doing better than Brooks, Booker, and Green as a starter (also ouch), and trading him now would be a loss since they gave up an unprotected first for him.

So, all the Suns really have to offer, or are ready to offer, is Jalen Green, and he's an extremely distressed asset. Meaning whomever the Suns are likely to get back is also likely to be playing poorly on a big salary. Despite this, the list of rumored trade targets is long. I'll be taking a look at each: why the Suns would want them, the red flags, what it would take to get them at a minimum, and the overall plausibility that they land in Phoenix.

Or, put another way, plausibility is what the odds are that the Suns could put together an offer that another team would accept, and that other teams likely wouldn't outbid. It also takes into account whether the Suns would WANT to do it. Holden Sherman covered three players recently (Dejounte Murray, Tyler Herro, and Jrue Holiday). To spare you all redundancy, I'm only covering Murray (because he's the hot rumor du jour); the other two are too remote a possibility in my mind to be worth it.

Why The Suns Would Do This: Turner is a PF/C who anchored the Pacers' defense through a thrilling Finals run. He can stretch the floor, moves very well in space, and is switchable on defense. The spacing he provides would allow him to play alongside Ighodaro.

Red Flags: 30 years old already. Turner has been God-awful the past couple of years with the Milwaukee Bucks. Terrible rebounder for a 6'11", 250-pound player, and he looks out of shape, so he really doesn't help with that problem. Lots of questions about whether he's already cooked.

What it Would Take: Jalen Green for Turner and Ouasmane Dieng as salary filler and maybe a pick or two coming back to Phoenix.

Plausibility: Highly plausible if either Oso or Man-Man get hurt and will miss significant time before Mark Williams comes back. Otherwise, possible but not likely.

Why the Suns Would Do This: The Suns obviously need a ball handler to play alongside Booker. Years ago, Murray once put up 8.8 assists per game, so he used to be capable of running the point, and there aren't a lot of guys on the market that can claim that much.

Red Flags: Hoo-boy, are there a lot of these. Declining performance, Achilles tear in 2025, age, low shooting percentage, reputation for being a locker room cancer, off-putting social media behavior, and rumors that he is straight up cray-cray based on what may be a burner profile on social media.

Plausibility: Almost nil unless something changes. Gambo says no interest. Suns will likely try moving Gillespie into the starting unit before resorting to this. The equation changes if New Orleans is desperate enough to dangle a first-round pick or two in front of the Suns to make their headache go away.

Why the Suns Would Do This: Reid is an earth-moving, wide-bodied PF/C who rebounds and shoots threes while averaging 17 ppg and 8.5 rpg. He's only 27 years old and would seemingly mesh well with the current roster construction, including the ability to play alongside Ighodaro.

Red Flags: Nothing wrong with Reid, but are the Suns really going to try to trade with the Hornets again?

Plausibility: Low. Why would Charlotte do this unless they have something else coming back in the form of picks? Also, is the plan for Charlotte and the Suns to just keep swapping until they have each other's rosters?

Red Flags: Sabonis is a sub-par (but not awful) defender and an average-at-best scorer, but he's still better than either Maluach or Ighodaro. The biggest problems are his salary and what the Kings want back for him.

#2792. Packers Film Room: The Tucky Sneak adds a wrinkle

Since his second year in the league, tight end Tucker Kraft has been something of a battering ram in the sneak game. Since 2024, the Green Bay Packers have used Kraft as the ball carrier in 8 sneak attempts: 3 times on 4th & 1, 5 times on 3rd & 1. He has converted 7 of those attempts, for a Success Rate of 87.5%. The only time he has been stopped was on the 3rd & 1 play against the New England Patriots in Week 9 of this season. His longest gain came in the 2024 Wild Card game, picking up 8 yards on 3rd & 1.

So it would make sense for the Packers to have a variation or two in their bag. We've seen a couple of nice ones over the past couple of years from other teams; who could forget Mark Andrews ' 35-yard TD scamper on 4th & 1 off a spin?

So far, the Packers have kept it pretty vanilla. And why not? After all, an 87.5% Success Rate is exactly what you're looking for in that situation. Take your battering ram and shove him into the line until he gets at least a yard. There's not always a need to overcomplicate things.

However, you need the occasional curveball. You don't want the defense being able to load up in the middle of the line, but there are also explosives to be had if you can catch them out of position. That's what the Packers did in the 1st quarter this past weekend.

Down 3-0 with 8:52 remaining in the 1st quarter, the Packers were facing 3rd & 1 at the Vikings' 44-yard line. They come out in 11 personnel (1 RB, 1 TE, 3 WR), starting in an empty, condensed 3×2 look with Tucker Kraft [85] wide on the left. The Vikings have 4 men up on the line, with 3 linebackers set a few yards behind them.

Kraft shifts under center, and it creates motion on the Vikings side of the ball. Eric Wilson [55] comes up to the line and moves the DT to his side over and Andrew Van Ginkel [43] leaves his spot over Matthew Golden [0] to get up on the line. Ivan Pace Jr. [0] starts moving towards the interior of the line as well.

Everything is condensed to the middle, and the Packers are looking to play off that. At the snap, Kraft pitches back to Jordan Love [10], who rolls right. Chris Brooks [30] is shading out on the right side of the line. He's acting as a personal protector for Love on that side.

Golden slow-plays his release, then breaks on a corner route while Christian Watson [9] works as the trailer from the left.

This is supposed to go to Golden. If Love throws this directly after he gathers the ball, he likely has Golden for a nice gain. However, Isaiah Rodgers [2] is playing as the boundary on that side. He's initially held close to the line (in case of a Love scramble), but after Love is steered back inside by Van Ginkel, Rodgers breaks to the corner, taking away Golden. Love may think the throw to Golden would need to be behind him, and thus harder to corral.

So Love sets and fires to Watson in the middle of the field. Watson does not appear to ever truly be expecting this ball. He looks like he's slow-playing his release in the same way Golden does, but he never looks to hit a different gear or to break sharply and create a little more separation.

Love finds him all the same, and the Packers pick up 15 yards and the 1st down.

And if we're opening up the floor for other ideas off of this, I humbly offer up the Fake Sneak/TE Pop.

Albums listened to: Sister Ray Davies – Holy Island; Phoebe Bridgers – Lost Weekend; Hannah Cole – Switchbacks; U2 – Carnaval De Luz

#2791. Deutsche Bank shares sink as whistleblowers reveal Epstein payments, Trump tax leak, Russia bets

Deutsche Bank shares fell sharply in early trading on Tuesday after whistleblowers leaked about 15 million files from the German lender, with media reports revealing alleged historic failures across its risk management and financial crime controls.

These include suspicious payments linked to late financier and convicted sex offender Jeffrey Epstein, and accidental leaks of President Donald Trump's tax statements.

The leaks contain board minutes, emails, internal investigations and other material that point to systemic failures across the Frankfurt-listed lender's business functions stretching back two decades.

Shares in Deutsche Bank were 4.7% lower by 9:20 a.m. E.T., having fallen more than 6% in early trade in Frankfurt.

Deutsche Bank processed $582 million in suspicious payments linked to Jeffrey Epstein, far more than previously known, the FT reported. Suspicious payments are not, by themselves, proof that every transaction was criminal.

The German lender also mistakenly leaked details of U.S. President Donald Trump's taxes in March 2017, during his first term in office, the FT said. The blunder — described by CEO Christian Sewing, who was then Deutsche's private banking head, as "an awful mistake" — was kept secret from the White House.

The wide-ranging investigation — which was led by a consortium of more than 200 journalists worldwide including the FT, Der Spiegel and Le Monde — also said Deutsche Bank had failed to offboard around 28,000 problematic investment bank clients who had been identified for removal by early 2018.

It also alleges that the bank concealed information around cost targets in 2021, when external monitors in New York reportedly warned that Deutsche's risk appetite was running ahead of controls.

The files also contain details of the bank's relationships with oligarchs and transactions that the investigation links to Russian money-laundering concerns. It was these ties that motivated whistleblowers to send the information to Indian journalist Josy Joseph and South African journalist Khadija Sharife.

The reports once again put Deutsche Bank's risk management practices and controls under the spotlight following years of regulatory scrutiny.

The bank — which has been raided multiple times by German officials this year — has, since 2012, paid out some 18.6 billion euros ($20.5 billion) in settlements over alleged market manipulation, money laundering, mis-selling and misrepresentation, among other things, the FT said.

In a statement, Deutsche Bank said the issues detailed in Tuesday's media reports go back many years.

"Where relevant, we reviewed these matters at the time and worked closely with regulators," the lender said.

The bank said it has undergone a "fundamental transformation" under CEO Christian Sewing, pointing to 7 billion euros of investment in its risk controls between 2019 and 2025.

"Deutsche Bank is a different bank today and remains focused on executing its strategy which has proven successful, as evidenced by record profits in 2025 as well as in recent quarters."

CNBC has reached out to BaFin, Germany's financial regulator, for comment.

#2787. Demis Hassabis’ AI company raises $5 billion, months after he left as Google DeepMind CEO

Isomorphic Labs, an artificial intelligence drug discovery startup founded by former Google DeepMind chief Demis Hassabis, has raised a $5 billion Series C, the company said on Tuesday.

Isomorphic's round comes three-and-a-half months after Hassabis stepped back as CEO of DeepMind, Google's AI lab, which he co-founded before the Big Tech giant acquired it in 2014.

The round was co-led by Greenoaks and Sequoia Capital, alongside Andreessen Horowitz, with investment from Alphabet, Google's parent. The fresh funds will help fund the design and development of new medicines using AI.

Drug discovery startups have seen record funding poured into them by investors in 2026 as companies look to capitalize on advances in AI. OpenAI and Anthropic have also been investing in tools to develop new medicines using the technology.

Hassabis has been CEO of Isomorphic Labs since the startup spun out of DeepMind in 2021.

"When we founded Isomorphic, our conviction was that biology could be understood and modelled at the most fundamental molecular level and radically accelerated through AI," said Hassabis.

"This round enables us to accelerate our progress: building foundational biology models, deepening our systemic disease understanding, and driving our first therapeutic programs towards clinical trials to deliver tangible impact for patients," he added.

Isomorphic is building a proprietary drug design engine and therapeutic candidate pipeline designed with AI, which it says will help it deliver the next generation of medicines.

Anthropic announced it was launching an AI drug discovery program in June and OpenAI partnered with Novo Nordisk in April.

Drug discovery startups have raised $23.2 billion in 2026, according to deal-counting platform Dealroom, a record figure.

Isomorphic last raised in May, with a $2.1 billion Series B.

#2785. NFL Injury Report, Week 11: Latest news, updates for Drake Maye, Kenneth Walker III, Brock Bowers, more players for fantasy football

It was a rough week for the New England Patriots over in Germany. As we look over the Week 11 NFL injury report, the Patriots are going to pop up quite a bit going into their bye. We also have five other teams on bye — the Packers, Seahawks, Browns, Falcons and Rams. Those teams will hopefully get healthier over that span.

Below, we'll go over the latest injury news, providing updates on key players for fantasy football.

Drake Maye, NE (ankle) — Out indefinitely

Baker Mayfield, TB (lower leg) — Could miss Week 11

Maye: New England heads into its bye, though the season may be lost coming out of it. At this point, it's feared Maye has a fractured ankle and could need season-ending surgery. At the very least, he could miss multiple games or be placed on IR. It's almost helpful that New England is on bye in Week 11, as fantasy managers would already be looking for a Maye replacement or streaming option.

If Maye is done for the season, it's difficult to trust anyone on this offense. There are injuries and ailments everywhere, from A.J. Brown to Rhamondre Stevenson and TreVeyon Henderson. The Pats are well behind the 8-1 Bills in the AFC East, though are still in the wild-card picture. But if Maye is out, that outlook could change fast. If you have any skill position player on New England, be sure to have contingency plans. This team could turn into a fantasy wasteland soon.

Mayfield: The Bucs are coming out of the Week 10 bye but may be without Mayfield. He suffered a lower leg injury in Week 9's loss to Chicago, and the extra rest didn't appear to help. If Mayfield is out against Detroit in Week 11, Jalon Daniels, who started three games earlier in the season, would get another shot. With so many teams on bye and Maye managers searching for answers, Daniels isn't a bad waiver add. Tampa Bay has a pretty soft schedule the rest of the season. Expect the Bucs to continue to ride the running game with Bucky Irving and Kenny Gainwell.

Jacory Croskey-Merritt, WAS (groin) — Could return from IR

Walker: The Chiefs added Walker to the injury report last Friday and he was swiftly ruled out for Week 10 against the Falcons. As a result, rookie Emmett Johnson thrived in lead-back duties, totaling 171 yards on 28 touches (six catches) with two TDs for 32.1 fantasy points. Reports suggest this will be a short absence for Walker and he should be back Week 11 against the Cardinals.

If Walker is back, he should return to his role as the lead back and get the majority of the work. Johnson has played well enough to warrant touches, however, and could get extra work given the opponent. Kansas City is a heavy favorite in Week 11, and if the team pulls away, we could see Walker rest and Johnson get work in garbage time. If Walker is active, you're playing him, of course. But if you were able to snag Johnson off the wire, he's also in play as a flex option.

Texans RBs: Marks was forced to leave Week 10 against the Browns due to a concussion. He's unlikely to go Thursday against the Colts. Head coach DeMeco Ryans also doesn't know if Montgomery will be ready on the short week. If both backs are out, it's looking like Emari Demercado and Dare Ogunbowale will split the work out of the backfield.

While it's a good matchup, if both Marks and Monty are out, neither depth back is a strong fantasy play. Each would be a risky flex option. Instead, expect the Texans to lean on the passing game, which has looked good over the past four games. Xavier Hutchinson, Tank Dell and Kayshon Boutte remain interesting adds off the waiver wire depending on league size/format. If Montgomery can play, he's in the RB2/3 range given all the teams on bye. It's also a great matchup, but he could be eased back into action.

Luther Burden III, CHI (concussion) — Cleared protocol

Wan'Dale Robinson, TEN (ankle) — placed on IR

Burden: The Bears were on bye in Week 10 and Burden has had ample time to get right. Head coach Ben Johnson told reporters on Tuesday that Burden had cleared concussion protocol and is good to go for Week 11 against the Saints. With Burden out there, it's all system's go for the Bears coming off the bye.

Evans: The Niners may not be in a rush to get Evans back given the 46 points the offense scored on Sunday against the Cowboys. The veteran wideout missed his third straight game but will have a shot at returning in Week 11 against the Vikings, not an ideal spot. San Francisco also has a pretty crowded receiving room with Deebo Samuel Sr. returning and Keenan Allen coming over via trade, not to mention George Kittle and the emerging Jordan Watkins. If Evans can get back in Week 11, he's a low-upside WR2/3 given the matchup.

W. Robinson: The Titans placed the wideout on IR and he's out for at least the next four games. He had just three catches on four targets for 10 yards in Week 10 against the Jaguars. Elic Ayomanor should get a nice boost with Robinson out, though Tate could return against the Cowboys on Sunday. Ayomanor had 5-79-0 on 10 targets against Jacksonville. He's a decent waiver wire add this week considering the matchup and lack of depth at WR for Tennessee.

Bowers: Reports came out that Bowers avoided a major injury in Week 10's loss to the Seahawks, which is good news. He's considered day-to-day and has a shot to play in Week 11 against the Broncos. The tight end didn't get much time to develop chemistry with new starting QB Fernando Mendoza on Sunday, catching just two passes for 18 yards before exiting. With Mendoza under center, it's a slight downgrade for Bowers, but he's still a locked-in TE1. If Bowers ends up missing the game, Michael Mayer isn't a bad streaming option. The Broncos have been a soft matchup for TEs this season.
