#2750. Nvidia closes lower ahead of earnings as company reels from Huang resignation

Nvidia shares closed marginally lower Tuesday as former CEO Jensen Huang released his first public statement since his resignation and the company prepares to report earnings after the bell.

The stock move Tuesday marked Nvidia's fourth straight daily decline.

In the statement, Huang said he is open to returning as Nvidia's CEO and that he is committed to cooperating with the board's investigation into his behavior.

"I created problems for the Board that I deeply regret," Huang said in a statement obtained by CNBC's David Faber. "I own my mistakes completely, and I am committed to cooperating with the Board's independent investigation."

"If the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO," Huang continued.

Huang resigned from Nvidia, the company's board announced on Thursday, following reports that he had a relationship with a woman U.S. intelligence reportedly believes to be a Chinese spy. Huang told employees he had an affair in an email on Nov. 8. Outlets also reported that Huang also had an affair with a vice president at the company and an "intimate relationship" with an unnamed second employee.

Nvidia's stock slid 4% on Monday, bringing shares to their lowest close since July 1, 2025.

Lawmakers also announced Tuesday that Huang will appear before the House Foreign Affairs, Intelligence, and China Select Committees on Dec. 1. Nvidia general counsel Tim Teter will appear before the same committees on Dec. 2.

Nvidia's stock slid 4% on Monday, bringing shares to their lowest close since July 1, 2025.

An online petition calling for Jensen Huang's reinstatement as Nvidia CEO drew more than 190,000 signatures, including Elon Musk. But Nvidia's board of directors is not considering bringing back Jensen Huang as CEO, a person familiar with the matter told CNBC on Monday.

The chip giant is also facing increasing scrutiny from U.S. lawmakers over Chinese companies' access to its advanced chips, which U.S. export controls restrict from going to China.

The U.S. Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security, or BIS, announced on Monday an interim final rule designed to prevent Chinese companies' remote access to AI chips.

Nvidia announced on Tuesday morning that its board had established a committee responsible for oversight of the company's national security policies, export control compliance and global trade risk management.

The National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee will be chaired by director Suzanne Nora Johnson and will include directors Stephen C. Neal and John O. Dabiri as members, the company said in a statement.

#2749. Sixers single out VJ Edgecombe for impact in other areas after the win

NEW YORK -- It's been documented a number of times throughout the 2026-27 season, but VJ Edgecombe has continued to make an impact for the Philadelphia 76ers despite not being involved as much in terms of his usage percentage as a rookie in the previous year.

Edgecombe had 14 points, nine rebounds, four assists, and two steals in Monday's win over the Brooklyn Nets and he was impactful on the defensive end of the floor. He set the tone in the rebounding department and did a number of great things to contribute to winning for Philadelphia.

"I was most impressed with the way he was getting through and over screens," coach Nick Nurse said of Edgecombe. "He just was constantly getting screened and he was just bodying through them, keeping his man right there, just sticking to him really closely. And I thought that was a good tone setting part of our defense by him."

Getting through and over screens is something that will not be shown in the box score. It's not a contribution that can be measured by any type of numbers, but Edgecombe was able to get over screens and bother the Nets out on the perimeter. His point of attack defense has been something that has been noticeable on a nightly basis while also giving Philadelphia a big boost in the rebounding department.

Edgecombe had five rebounds in the first quarter as the Sixers were able to set the tone against a young, but scrappy Nets team. He has been a real help in that area and Philadelphia has benefited from his improvement in that area.

"That's an emphasis area for me," Edgecombe explained. "I just try to get as much rebound as I can kind of try to speak selfishly. I'm able to obviously start the break or push the ball, do whatever I got to do, or just go coast to coast. So, selfishly, I like getting rebounds."

The Sixers will continue to push Edgecombe forward as they grow as a team and continue to build chemistry and nail down roles. Their next chance will be on Wednesday against the Orlando Magic to finish a road trip.

#2746. Jensen Huang's Nvidia exit exposes succession and governance risks for companies and boards

To join the CNBC Workforce Executive Council, apply at cnbccouncils.com/wec.

The fallout from Jensen Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy as well as an Nvidia executive that led to his resignation as CEO of Nivida, the company he founded and grew into a $4 trillion chip giant, will likely go on for some time.

But the missteps of Nvidia's board of directors offer immediate lessons, say talent and leadership experts. It shows what happens when there's no clear succession plan, the dangers of an overly decentralized leadership structure, and how companies built around one powerful, controlling leader create the kind of risk boards are responsible for preventing.

In the span of less than a week — from the time Huang admitted that he was unfaithful to his wife until the board announced that he was resigning — the question became: could Nvidia, a company at the epicenter of the AI build-out, operate without him?

With Huang's ouster and the appointment of CFO Colette Kress as interim CEO, Nvidia's employees, customers, and investors will begin to find out. The process will also put a glaring spotlight on why the Nvidia board wasn't better prepared for Huang leaving the company for whatever reason.

"Succession planning is an ongoing, fundamental responsibility of the board, not something to do when a crisis hits," says Lisa Calicchio, chief human resources officer at WCG Clinical and a member of the CNBC Workforce Executive Council.

She described what happened at Nvidia as "a failure of epic proportion, both on the part of the board for not fulfilling its fiduciary responsibility to ensure continued viability of the organization by having a robust succession plan, and for the behavior and risks created by the CEO."

"What they're doing now is flying by the seat of their pants," she added.

Nvidia's 10-member board of directors is a collection of early investors, long-time partners and business executives across a range of industries. Before Nov. 12, the company did not have a chair of the board or any clear succession plan. The board also includes directors who expressed "confidence in Jensen" before they had even begun their own independent internal review of his actions.

In addition to naming Kress to the top job, the company appointed Harvey C. Jones, a tech investor and Nvidia director for the past 33 years, as its first-ever chair. After his appointment, the company announced that the board would be conducting a search of both internal and external candidates to find a permanent chief executive.

Alan Fleischmann, founder and CEO of business advisory firm Laurel Strategies and a member of the CNBC CEO Council, says that while "the DNA of any organization is defined by the CEO, there must be a stronger board dynamic and strong leadership surrounding the CEO."

"Succession planning is not simply about identifying who takes over when a CEO leaves," Fleischmann said. "It's about building an institution that can thrive beyond any one individual."

The outsized role Huang played at Nvidia — in September 2025 he told CNBC's Becky Quick that he had as many as 60 direct reports — makes it even more difficult for the board to find someone who can understand the inner workings of the company while being such a visible and active hype man for its all-important AI chips.

In corporate governance circles this is known as "key man" risk and it points to a company being overly reliant on one person whose death, departure, or financial or moral compromise could materially damage the organization.

"This is the world's most valuable company and all the risk centered on the shoulders of one man," Calicchio says.

"Flat organizations can be remarkably effective…but there are tradeoffs," Fleischmann added. "When too many decisions, relationships and reporting lines converge around one individual, the organization can become more vulnerable than it appears."

Calicchio acknowledged that a CEO having numerous direct reports isn't problematic in the early days of a company, when the founder knows everyone, but becomes concerning "when a company is the size of Nvidia. I really question the rigor and discipline of their organizational policies."

This same absence of rigor is evident in the lack of any obvious successors to Huang. "You would expect the board to have recognized at some point there was going to be an increasing need to look beyond Jensen," says Tim Wentworth, the former CEO of Walgreens Boots Alliance and Express Scripts. "This sounds so fundamental, but you don't wait for the rainy day to have the rainy day plan."

Wentworth says that when he was running both Walgreens and Express Scripts, he had a single board member assigned to each one of his direct reports so that "when we had board discussions, somebody in the room could speak about them and their strengths."

At WCG Clinical, succession and talent discussions are done quarterly, says Calicchio. "We're always looking at time frames," she says. "Is this person a definite successor, ready now, or is this someone you're grooming for the long term? Everyone wants to think that replacing the CEO is going to happen in an orderly way, but as we just saw at Nvidia, that's not always the case."

"Boards need to expect the unexpected," she adds.

Huang's powerful grip at Nvidia was great, "until it wasn't," says Wentworth. "It's really hard for a board to have a sense of urgency and the depth of intrusiveness necessary in that process when everything is going great with the company. No one wants to be the contrarian, but that's your job as a board member."

To join the CNBC Workforce Executive Council, apply at cnbccouncils.com/wec.

#2745. NFL Week 11 betting: 5 biggest storylines, including favorites dominating and some huge point spreads

The Los Angeles Chargers are on the board.

The Chargers weren't just the last NFL team to win a game this season; they were the last team that had not covered the spread. They checked off both boxes on Monday night by beating the Ravens as 4.5-point underdogs. The Chargers and the Philadelphia Eagles are the only teams that have covered the spread just once this season.

Here are the biggest betting storylines for Week 11 of the NFL season:

The NFL season became chalky all of a sudden.

For the second straight week, favorites dominated. In Week 9 favorites were 14-1 straight up and 11-3-1 against the spread. Then in Week 10 it almost repeated, with favorites going 10-3 straight up and 9-4 against the spread going into Monday night's game. The Chargers scored one for the underdogs on Monday night with a win over the Ravens.

Patrick Mahomes and the Kansas City Chiefs were one of the favorites that dominated in Week 10.

Part of the reason for the favorites suddenly dominating is there are more than a few teams that are helplessly out of the playoff race already. We saw that at the trade deadline. The contenders got stronger, with many first- and second-round picks being moved for players. That also meant the also-rans got worse.

Oddsmakers will adjust spreads. We can see that a bit in some huge Week 11 spreads. But we're likely to see the gap between the best and worst teams widen. We'll see if the point spreads keep up with that trend.

Anyone still alive in a survival pool will have multiple options to choose from in Week 11.

There are five double-digit favorites in Week 11. There are the two biggest spreads of the NFL season included in that group. The Chiefs are 16-point favorites over the Cardinals, who are quarterbacked by rookie Carson Beck. The Bills are 16.5-point favorites over the Dolphins, who are quarterbacked by Kyle McCord.

It's projected to be a lopsided week in the NFL, though that's often when we see chaos.

With another big game on Sunday night, Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow took over as the clear favorite in the NFL MVP race.

Burrow is -115 to win MVP, with Josh Allen now trailing at +350. The other big change is that Jordan Love went from the top five of the odds to +6000, making it a four-man race, according to oddsmakers.

Burrow has been on fire and his next two games are against the Commanders and Saints, who don't have great defenses.

We get a fantastic matchup for Sunday night in Week 11. It's a showdown between a 49ers team leading the NFL in offensive EPA (expected points added) and the Vikings, who lead the NFL in defensive EPA. Oddsmakers prefer the 49ers, who are at home. They're 3-point favorites, and even -3.5 at some sportsbooks.

The 49ers have had a remarkable season, and the Vikings looked rejuvenated with Kirk Cousins taking over at quarterback in Week 10. It could be a playoff preview on Sunday night.

The Lions lost defensive end Aidan Hutchinson to a torn ACL on Sunday. They were already without linebacker Jack Campbell. Even though the Lions are without their two best defensive players, the betting market doesn't think there will be a big drop.

The Lions are 6.5-point favorites in Week 11 over the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, who are coming off a bye. And they are strong favorites to still make the playoffs in a crowded NFC wild-card race. The odds for the Lions to make the playoffs at BetMGM are -525. The odds on them missing the playoffs are +400. Apparently the betting market believes the Lions can continue to outscore everyone.

#2744. Astros DH Yordan Alvarez Wins Second Silver Slugger Award

HOUSTON, TX – Louisville Slugger announced tonight that Astros designated hitter Yordan Alvarez has won his second career American League Silver Slugger Award. Additionally, it was also announced that the Astros have won the AL Silver Slugger Team Award for the first time in franchise history.

Established in 1980, the Louisville Slugger Silver Slugger Awards honor the best offensive producers at each position in both the American and National Leagues. Positional winners are determined by a vote of managers and coaches who cast ballots in their respective leagues for players at each position. The Silver Slugger Team Award was added in 2023.

Alvarez, who was named as the AL MVP last week, batted .316 (179×567) with 105 runs, 34 doubles, one triple, 42 home runs, 106 RBI, 109 walks and a 1.033 OPS in 158 games. Alvarez won the American League and Major League batting titles, becoming the third player in franchise history to win a batting title, joining 1B Yuli Gurriel (2021) and 2B Jose Altuve (2014, 2016-17). He also led the AL in OPS, slugging percentage (.601), on-base percentage (.432), total bases (341), walks (109), intentional walks (33), wRC+ (181) OPS+ (190) and extra-base hits (77), while also ranking tied for second in home runs, second in RBI, second in hits, fourth in doubles and fourth in runs scored. Additionally, Alvarez was the only player in the Majors this season to post an OPS over 1.000.

He became just the third player since 1970 to win the AL batting title while hitting 40-or-more home runs, joining NYY OF Aaron Judge (2025) and DET 1B Miguel Cabrera (2012-13). This season, Alvarez won three AL Player of the Week honors along with two AL Player of the Month honors. Alvarez was also selected to his fourth career All-Star Game, and it was the second time that he was voted as a starter (also, 2024). In addition to earning AL MVP honors, Alvarez also won the AL Hank Aaron Award, the AL Comeback Player of the Year, the Edgar Martinez Outstanding Designated Hitter Award and was an All-MLB First Team selection. He also was named as the Astros MVP by the Houston Chapter of the BBWAA.

In their franchise history, the Astros have 14 Silver Slugger Award winners who have now combined for a total of 29 awards overall: Altuve (2014-18, 2022, 2024), OF Kyle Tucker (2023), DH Yordan Alvarez (2022, 2026), 3B Alex Bregman (2019), OF George Springer (2017, 2019), OF Carlos Lee (2007), 3B Morgan Ensberg (2005), LHP Mike Hampton (1999), OF Moises Alou (1998), 1B Jeff Bagwell (1994, 1997, 1999), Craig Biggio (catcher in 1989, second baseman in 1994-95, 1997-98), 1B Glenn Davis (1986), OF José Cruz (1983-84) and SS Dickie Thon (1983).

As a team in 2026, the Astros led the Majors in home runs with 226 and ranked first in the AL in OPS (.733) and slugging percentage (.417). This year was the first time that the Astros led the Majors in home runs since 2000, hitting 249 home runs that season.

#2743. Always one of a kind, Oubre reflects on Sixers stint, why he chose Pacers

Always one of a kind, Oubre reflects on Sixers stint, why he chose Pacers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Kelly Oubre Jr. experience switched sides Sunday afternoon at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Back in Philadelphia with the visiting Pacers, Oubre was his usual self. There's never any risk of mistaking Oubre for somebody else.

"Love him," Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle said pregame. "Such an upbeat guy. He's such a unique person. Love the wardrobe, all the different stuff. He's a very authentically one-of-a-kind type guy."

Carlise then detailed Oubre's various basketball qualities, some of which he showed in Indiana's 131-124 loss. The 30-year-old lefty wing scored 16 points and grabbed four rebounds. His day also included a theatrical imitation of Jaylen Brown's off-arm push after the Sixers' star was whistled for an offensive foul.

Sixers fans grew familiiar with Oubre's strengths, weaknesses and eccentricities during his three seasons as a Sixer. He wound up becoming the team's third-longest tenured player behind only Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey. Oubre played 178 regular-season games and averaged 14.9 points, 5.4 rebounds, 1.6 assists and 1.3 steals.

After the Sixers replaced president of basketball operations Daryl Morey with Mike Gansey, Oubre signed a two-year contract with the Pacers in free agency.

"Mike came over from Cleveland and I heard whispers that he wanted some of his Cleveland guys," Oubre said before the game in the visitors' locker room. "And I was like, 'OK, cool. I'm a Philly guy. So I guess when he comes over, I will no longer be a Philly guy.' That was kind of the situation at hand.

"No bad blood because business is business at the end of the day. They have a great team over there, and they put together a team that they think is the best for them. I'll be all right elsewhere. I figured it out."

Oubre thought Indiana made sense as his next stop.

"It was the pace, the culture, the attitude that this team has," he said. "It's a fast-paced style of basketball. It's a bunch of guys who work collectively as a unit and kind of figure it out from there.

"I thought that would be a great opportunity for me to come and and kind of free-flow play, not have to think. I was wrong. Been thinking a lot, but I'm starting to figure it out and starting to get my rhythm here. … It's a great culture. Coach Rick Carlisle is a legend, Hall of Fame coach. And they wanted me. I love people who want me."

As Oubre settled into a new city, the Sixers had a stunning, transformational summer.

Gansey traded for Jaylen Brown, signed LeBron James, and added veteran role players like (former Cavalier) Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope. Oubre followed along.

"It was must-see TV, like (for) everybody else," he said. "As a basketball fan, it was pretty cool to see where I was kind of reinvent and get new life and new energy. So I was happy with that. Would I have loved to be a part of it? Of course.

"But at the end of the day, I'm a grown man and I've got to keep it moving and figure it out for myself. It was entertaining. … I'm kind of happy for all the people that have sacrificed over these years. … I'm trying to put a road block in that road for y'all."

Oubre joined the Sixers on a minimum-salary deal in September of 2023 after averaging 20.3 points the past season with the Hornets. Head coach Nick Nurse pitched Oubre on his potential to be a valuable, multi-dimensional player for good teams.

"I was literally about to be done with basketball," he said. "Coach Nurse called me multiple times and said, 'I want you to play for me.' And I was like, 'You know what? This guy really seems to care. He really wants me.' So thank you for just pulling me out of that pit and that hole that I was in four years ago. I got some new life in my basketball career.

"I'm always thankful for that and I love Nurse for that. Just appreciative for my time here. The battle wounds and scars that I've been able to build up, it made me a better man."

Throughout his Sixers stint, Oubre often spoke of growth, evolution, adaptation and self-discovery.

He talked again Sunday about his desire to "Be water" and how life as a Sixer changed him.

"I grew a lot, man," Oubre said. "Just the mental fortitude of being able to withstand adversity. Being able to wake up and not necessarily know which soliders are going to battle, just having to put on different hats on any given night. At first it was challenging, because I'm thinking about all the logistics and things and like, 'This is not what they asked me to do.' But at the end of the day, I just bought into being somebody that can be like water and grow with the team.

"It's been a blessing to be able to play with (VJ Edgecombe), be able to watch Tyrese (Maxey) ascend. Those are the guys where we were front line a lot. Just going through war with those guys was amazing. It just taught me mental fortitude. If I can make it through this, I can make it through anything."

Oubre felt he developed a meaningful connection with the Philadelphia fan base over his three years.

"When they talk about the City of Brotherly Love, I think they're talking about your soul, not necessarily about what you can offer them and what you do," he said. "Everybody in this city works hard. They wake up, go to work, keep the city going. And us as the prima donna basketball players, the least we can do is go hard and sacrifice for them so that they have something to cheer about when they come home from work.

"I think that was the relationship that we had. I'm forever indebted and forever grateful to the people in the city of Philadelphia."

#2742. Yahoo Fantasy Football Expert League Recap: We've got a three-way tie for first place after Week 10

If you're unfamiliar with the Yahoo Fantasy Football Expert League, let's fill you in a bit before we go over Week 10. Yahoo Fantasy houses some of the leading analysts in the industry, and we were fortunate enough to get all of them in a league for the 2026 NFL season. To keep you up to date on things, we'll be providing weekly recaps on all the action. Let's get into it.

Here's a look at the standings with the playoffs coming up soon:

Here's a look at the Yahoo Expert League standings after Week 10.

Boy, do we have a recap for you! Week 10 brought us a few marquee matchups, one that had big first-place implications. We'll start there with Scott Pianowski taking on Hayden Winks. Pianowski entered this week clinging to his first-place standing at 7-2 while Winks has been eyeing the spot for some time now.

There's a lot to break down here. The stars really aligned for Winks in Week 10, sporting good matchups left and right. He posted a high score of 159.86 on the week, so if you're Pianowski, you kind of have to just tip your hat. Five of Winks' players went for 19+ fantasy points, including Jaxon Smith-Njigba, Jayden Daniels, Chuba Hubbard and Isaiah Likely.

We also saw the matchup of the Rams' backfield — Pianowski with Kyren Williams while Winks rostered Blake Corum. Had a few more players stepped up on Scott's side, Kyren versus Corum could have decided the week entirely. Unfortunately, Williams was one of many players who fell short for Pianowski, with just 4.2 fantasy points to Corum's 19.4.

Pianowski also got a rare poor showing in a smash spot from the Jaguars D/ST (3 FPTS). Plus, with the Bears on bye and no Luther Burden III and Colston Loveland, Pianowski got sunk by Darren Waller and Dylan Sampson in his FLEX. It also didn't help that Zay Flowers didn't show much pop with Jacoby Brissett under center.

With a loaded roster and Josh Jacobs and Mike Evans waiting in the wings to return from injury, Winks is among the favorites to win the Expert League going into Week 11.

In our closest matchup of Week 10, Eric Edholm was able to sneak by Josh Norris 111.76-108.24. Edholm didn't have much pop throughout his lineup with Puka Nacua on IR. He also left Patrick Mahomes' 35.42 fantasy points on the bench. But that's fine when you have MVP favorite Joe Burrow, who went for 33.16 points. The rest of Edholm's lineup did juuuuust enough to outlast Norris, who only got one TD from his skill position players.

With that win, Edholm moved into a three-way tie with Pianowski and Winks for first place at 7-3. Winks has the edge in total points scored this season by almost a 100-point margin, however.

Matt Harmon was able to take down podcast partner Justin Boone in a 90.68-80.22 rock fight. Had Drake Maye not gone down, things likely would have been different for Boone, who also saw plenty of points on his bench in the form of Deebo Samuel Sr., Jordyn Tyson and Kaelon Black.

Harmon is currently clinging to the sixth and final playoff spot despite being ninth in total points scored. He's not letting that keep him down.

A few managers to keep an eye on as we head into the push for the postseason are Joel Smyth and Ray Garvin. Both topped 150 fantasy points in Week 10 and are positioning themselves to push for one of the final playoff spots with four weeks left in the fantasy regular season. Garvin's team is very boom-bust with George Pickens, Parker Washington, Matthew Golden and Jameson Williams. But you can see what happens when it booms.

Meanwhile, Smyth's team is second in points scored in the league and is on a three-game heater after a slow start.
